2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
 隨著法國距離下屆總統大選僅剩六個月，極左翼與政府間的政治對立升溫，然而
法國政府急需推動預算案以控制財政赤字與龐大債務。在內政危機加劇與工會
預告 11月大罷工的背景下，市場對法國債務可持續性的擔憂升溫，將帶動法德
公債利差維持高檔，進而推升歐元區主權風險溢酬並拖累歐元表現。
 上週路透最新調查顯示，在 100 位受訪經濟學家中，高達 94%預期聯準會將於
10 月會議維持利率在 3.75%-4.00% 不變，惟全員一致預估 12 月將重啟升息。
儘管美國受 AI 投資熱潮帶動、經濟成長強勁，且美伊戰事推升油價突破百元
大關、加徵關稅使 PCE 通膨至少在 2028 年前難以回到 2% 目標，官員仍傾
向審慎行事。不過逾四成專家警告，聯準會恐落後通膨形勢，後續面臨至少再升
息兩次甚至擴大升息幅度的上行風險，預期美元大幅回檔的空間小，逢低承接買
盤依然強勁。
 路透最新調查顯示，受日圓疲軟與中東戰事推升能源及進口成本、加劇通膨壓力

影響，近七成受訪經濟學家預計日本央行將於 12月再度升息至 1.50%，延續 6
月與 9 月以來的較快緊縮步伐；同時，逾半數專家預估終端利率將上看 2.00%
或更高。不過，高達近四分之三受訪者警告，若高市早苗內閣提名的鴿派委員再
次投下反對票，恐壓抑日圓表現。在歐美央行年底前亦可能升息的背景下，日本
央行維持較快的緊縮節奏，有助於防止美日、歐日利差進一步擴大，避免日圓在
年底遭遇新一輪劇烈拋售，為日圓提供實質的下檔保護。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Oct/27/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週台幣匯價高點為 32.360，低點在 32.704，以 32.508作收，較前一周收盤價
貶值 1 角 7 分。 
上週台幣走貶，周初，外資延續前周大幅匯出的走勢，台幣跳空開低，跌破今
年 7 月的低點 32.528；隨著台股表現不佳，外資賣超台股並匯出，台幣續創低位，
最低來到 32.704周內低點；而後台股低檔反彈，外資由匯出轉為雙向進出，加以出
口商積極進場拋售美金，台幣由貶轉升至 32.500下方；臨近周末，市場擔憂颱風來
襲周五可能休市，增添連續假期變數，外資再度大幅賣超台股並匯出，令台幣又跌
回 32.600上方，終場接在央行調節之下收在 32.508。 
＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣走勢偏弱。近期全球科技股因 AI 類股估值偏高及融資風險疑慮
而持續修正，市場風險偏好降溫，股市面臨修正，外資連續賣超台股並匯出，台幣
走勢持續面臨壓力。另一方面，美伊戰事和談進展的消息反覆，令國際油價節節攀
升，突破每桶 110美元上方，引發通膨升溫的擔憂，支撐利率維持在高檔的預期，
美元指數也在 101.5 上方高位震盪，將不利台幣的表現。盤勢上來看，外資隨著股
市回檔修正表現偏向賣超台股並匯出，台幣偏弱，不過本周適逢月底，預計匯價逢
高仍持續有出口商拋售美金的需求，將限制台幣走貶的幅度。綜合以上，預估本週
台幣走勢偏弱。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：32.300-32.800

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Oct/27/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣走升。周初中國公布9月工業生產物價指數 5.5%，表現優於預期的
5.4%(前值 5.2%)，且第三季 GDP表現 4.5%，也優於預期的 4.4%(前值 4.3%)，基本
面穩健支撐人民幣升破 6.6800 整數關口；不過周間美元指數持續於 101.5 上方高位
震盪，加以美伊戰事未見緩解，國際油價持續飆升，突破每桶 110 美元上方，人民
幣於 6.6800下方高檔震盪；臨近周末，人行中間價格連續走升，升破6.7200整數關
口，週五最高來到 6.7180，顯示中國人行允許人民幣溫和升值的走勢，終場境外人
民幣升破 6.6700整數關口 ， 收在6.6690， 週線升值160點 ， 境內人民幣則收在6.6704，
週線升值 166 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣偏升。就中國國內市場來看，人行中間價持續偏升，將持續帶
動人民幣走升，加以出口商實質拋匯需求將支撐人民幣表現；就國際市場來看，最
新利率期貨顯示 ， 本周聯準會利率決議有9成的機率維持利率在 3.75%~4.00%不變 ，
不過年底前仍有一碼的升息空間，加以地緣政治風險未見緩解，能源價格高檔震盪
推升通膨與利率預期，美元指數維持在 101.5 上方高檔震盪，將限制人民幣升值的
速度跟幅度；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH日線圖中，各天期均線向下發散，匯價
維持在五日均線之下交投，均線呈現空頭排列，顯示空方勢力較盛，綜合以上，預
估本週人民幣偏升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6300-6.6900

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Oct/27/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 USD/JPY 震盪下跌。週初，USD/JPY 大致在 158 的整數關卡上下波動；
週間，AI相關概念股遭拋售，加之中東局勢緊張推升避險需求，10年期美債殖利率
跌破 5%，避險資金亦流入日圓，帶動 USD/JPY 下行最低來至 156.67；臨近週末，
市場避險需求持續加之日本 9 月通膨數據高於日本央行 2%目標，USD/JPY 一度下
跌至 156.06，後逢低買盤進場跌幅有所收斂，終場收在 156.86，週線下跌 113點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY區間震盪，主要原因如下： 一、美國10 月 S&P製造業 PMI
為 56.0高於 9 月的 55.9且為 53個月高點，服務業則為 57.9低於 9月的 58.8，綜合
PMI為 57.6亦低於 9 月的 58.4，顯示美國商業活動雖略有放緩但仍處於擴張，經濟
基本面依然穩健將持續支撐美元；此外，中東局勢惡化使能源供應風險上升，布蘭
特原油價格突破 116美元，為六個月以來高點，也將有利USD/JPY上行。二、日本
9 月全國整體消費者物價指數（CPI）年增 2.1%，且 9 月核心 CPI年增 2.1%，為九
個月以來首次超過日本央行 2%的目標，強化市場對日本央行短期升息的預期，預計
為日圓帶來支撐。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY匯價處於各天期均線之
下且接連跌破 158 及 157 關卡，後在 156 整數關卡暫時獲得支撐，顯示逢低仍有買
盤進場，將限縮下行幅度。綜合以上，預估USD/JPY本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較
高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：155.00-158.50 
前週交易區間 
156.06 158.59

### Page 6

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
         Oct/27/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 EUR/USD震盪下跌。週初，EUR/USD在 1.1200整數關卡窄幅波動；週間
受到油價及美債殖利率回落，外加法國社會局勢略見緩解影響，帶動匯價反彈至
1.1260遇到賣壓後回落，後續大致在1.1190至 1.1250間波動；臨近週末，歐元區10
月份 PMI表現低迷，且市場對於法國政局和財政赤字擔憂持續，拖累 EUR/USD轉
若，匯價一度下行來至 1.1183，終場收在 1.1202，週線下跌 37點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD震盪偏空，主要原因如下：一、上週公佈歐元區10月 PMI
顯示德、法表現分化，法國製造業及服務業均表現疲弱低於50榮枯線，拖累歐元區
10 月綜合 PMI 由 9 月 53.1 下滑至 51.9，為四個月以來新低，經濟基本面承壓將不
利 EUR/USD上行；此外，法國社會及政經局勢不穩導致法債殖利率突破5%，市場
對其財政及政治危機擔憂持續，也將不利歐元表現。二、美國上週初請失業金人數
降至 19.4萬人，為三個月以來新低，顯示勞動力市場依然穩健，且10月 S&P製造
業 PMI為 56.0續創 53個月新高，服務業及綜合PMI維持穩健，顯示美國經濟仍具
韌性，預期將限縮 EUR/USD上行空間。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD
各天期均線維持空頭排列，且匯價持續承壓於月線之下，顯示短線空方趨勢仍未改
變，在能有效突破上方月線前仍以震盪偏空看待。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週
呈現震盪偏空的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1120-1.1270 
前週交易區間 
1.1180 1.1260

### Page 7

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
新台幣利率走勢分析

115.10.19-115.10.22  
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.825%  
30天期短票利率   1.67% ~ 1.68%  
  公債－10年期- A15109 2.000%~2.002% 
      － 5 年期- A15108     1.88%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分，儘管市場預期強烈颱風「薔琵」來襲，週五金融市場可能
因颱風假而暫停交易，銀行間多預留帳上資金以應不時之需，但市況仍屬充
裕，即便有新增調度需求，也很容易滿足，令短率朝區間低位移動。 
週四隔夜拆款利率區間在 0.805%~0.825%， 30 天期短票主要成交在
1.65%~1.68%，利率低位較上週下跌約 1 個基本點。

二、 本週市場交投冷清，10 年期指標券 A15109 週四於等殖系統成交在 2.002%，
5 年期指標券 A15108則未成交，最近成交利率為 1.88%。 
週二 10 年期公債標售得標利率 2.001%，落於市場預期區間下緣，並創 2008
年以來新高，顯示市場持續反映經濟成長與通膨預期。 
考量通膨前景及國際債市走勢仍具不確定性，市場觀望氣氛濃厚；惟第四季公
債供給相對有限，可望抑制殖利率大幅彈升。預期短線台債殖利率維持區間整
理，5 年券及 10 年券交易區間分別估於 1.82%~1.84%及 1.98%~2.01%。

## Document 058: 2026年7-9月期の実質GDP～前期比▲0.1％（年率▲0.3％）を予測～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年7-9月期の実質GDP～前期比▲0.1％（年率▲0.3％）を予測～.pdf
Report source: NLI

Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

実質ＧＤＰ成長率の推移
民間最終消費支出 民間企業設備 その他民需 公需 外需
（資料）内閣府経済社会総合研究所「四半期別ＧＤＰ速報」
( )内は年率換算値
(注）2602までは当研究所による改定見込値、2603は予測値
予測

１. 11/16に内閣府から公表される 2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDPは、前期比▲0.1％（前期比年
率▲0.3％）と 4四半期ぶりのマイナス成長になったと推計される。 
２. 高水準の企業収益を背景に設備投資が前期比 1.4％と大幅に増加したが、天候不順の影響
などから民間消費が前期比▲0.3％と減少したこと、原油を中心に輸入が大幅に増加し、
外需が成長率を大きく押し下げたことなどから、小幅ながら 4四半期ぶりのマイナス成
長になったとみられる。 
３. 名目 GDPは前期比 0.5％（前期比年率2.0％）と 10四半期連続で増加し、実質GDPの伸
びを大きく上回るだろう。GDPデフレーターは前期比 0.6％、前年比 2.5％と予測する。 
４. 2026年 7-9月期のマイナス成長の一因となった民間消費の減少は、天候不順による一時
的な影響が大きい。ただし、こうした影響が解消されても、物価上昇の加速が消費の回
復を妨げる可能性が高い。 
５. 現時点では、2026年 10-12月期はプラス成長に復帰するものの、民間消費が伸び悩むこ
となどから、前期比年率 0％台半ばとマイナス成長の後としては低めの伸びにとどまる
と予想している。 
ニッセイ基礎研究所 2026-10-30

2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDP 
～前期比▲0.1％（年率▲0.3％）を予測～

経済研究部 経済調査部長 斎藤  太郎 
(03)3512-1836  tsaito@nli-research.co.jp

### Page 2

名目 GDPは前期比 0.5％（前期比年率 2.0％）と 10四半期連続で増加し、実質 GDPの伸びを大
きく上回るだろう。GDPデフレーターは前期比 0.6％（4-6月期：同 1.0％）、前年比 2.5％（4-6月
期：同 2.6％）と予測する。

なお、11/16に内閣府から 2026年 7-9月期の GDP速報が発表される際には、基礎統計の改定や季
節調整のかけ直しなどから、成長率が過去に遡って改定される。当研究所では、2026年 4-6月期の
実質 GDP成長率は、外需、設備投資が上方修正されることから、前期比年率 1.4％から同 2.3％へ
上方修正されると予想している。

2026年 7-9月期のマイナス成長の一因となった民間消費の減少は、天候不順による一時的な影響
が大きい。ただし、こうした影響が解消されても、物価上昇の加速が消費の回復を妨げる可能性が
高い。消費者物価上昇率は 2026年 9月に 9ヵ月ぶりの 2％台となったが、原材料価格の上昇を耐久
財や日用品などの販売価格に転嫁する動きが今後さらに広がることから、2026年度末にかけて 3％
台まで高まる公算が大きい。物価上昇率の高まりによる実質購買力の低下を背景に 2026年度後半
の民間消費は弱めの動きが続くことが予想される。 
現時点では、2026年 10-12月期はプラス成長に復帰するものの、民間消費が伸び悩むことなどか
ら、前期比年率 0％台半ばとマイナス成長の後としては低めの伸びにとどまると予想している。

1 10/30までに公表された経済指標をもとに予測している。今後公表される経済指標の結果によって予測値を修正する可
能性がある。

### Page 3

民間消費は前期比▲0.3％と予測する。 
実質賃金はプラスの伸びが続いているが、物価上昇率の高まりによって実質購買力が押し下げら
れていることに加え、台風や豪雨、長雨などの天候不順が個人消費を下押しした。 
2026年 7-9月期の消費関連指標を確認すると、延べ宿泊者数（うち日本人）は前期比 2.8％（4-6
月期：同▲0.9％）の増加となったが、自動車税の
「環境性能割」の廃止を受けて 4-6月期に前期比
10.7％の高い伸びとなった自動車販売台数が、その
反動で同▲7.5％と落ち込んだほか、外食産業売上
高が前期比▲0.3％（4-6月期：同 0.2％）、小売業
販売額指数が前期比▲2.1％（4-6月期：同 0.6％）
と減少した（いずれもニッセイ基礎研究所による
季節調整値、外食産業売上高、小売業販売額指数
は消費者物価指数で実質化）。

・住宅投資～弱めの動きが続く～

住宅投資は前期比▲0.2％と 2四半期連続の減
少を予測する。 
新設住宅着工戸数（季節調整済・年率換算値）
は、建築物省エネ法・建築基準法改正前の駆け
込み需要の反動で、2025年 1-3月期の 86.7万戸
から 4-6月期に 62.0万戸へ急減した後、7-9月
が 71.9万戸と持ち直し、その後は概ね 70万戸
台半ばで推移している。 
GDP統計の住宅投資は住宅価格上昇の影響も
あり弱めの動きとなっている。

・民間設備投資～高水準の企業収益を背景に増加～

民間設備投資は前期比 1.4％と 3四半期ぶりの増加を予測する。 
設備投資の一致指標である資本財総供給は 2026年 4-6月期の前期比 4.9％の後、7-9月期も同 4.9％
となった。また、機械投資の先行指標である機械受注（船舶・電力を除く民需）は 2026年 4-6月
期に前期比 0.2％と 3四半期連続で増加した後、7、8月の平均は 4-6月期を 0.4％上回っている。 
日銀短観 2026年 9月調査では、2026年度の設備投資計画（含むソフトウェア・研究開発投資額、
除く土地投資額）が6月調査から 0.5％上方修正され、前年度比9.3％（全規模・全産業）となった。 
設備投資は、高水準の企業収益を背景に、人手不足対応の省力化投資、デジタル化に向けた情報

・公的固定資本形成～2四半期連続の減少～

公的固定資本形成は、前期比▲2.4％と予測する。 
公共工事の先行指標である公共工事請負金額
は 2026年 7-9月期に前年比▲2.6％と 6四半期ぶ
りに減少した。また、公共工事の進捗を反映する
公共工事出来高（建設総合統計）は、2026年 4-6
月期に前年比 5.2％と 9四半期連続で増加した後、
2026年 7、8月の平均は同 2.4％と伸びが鈍化し
ている。 
2025年度補正予算による押し上げ効果が弱ま
っていることに加え、2026 年度当初予算の公共
事業関係費の伸びが抑えられていることから、弱
めの動きとなっている。

・外需～4四半期ぶりのマイナス寄与～

外需寄与度は前期比▲0.4％（前期比年率▲1.8％）と 4四半期ぶりのマイナスを予測する。財貨・
サービスの輸出が前期比 2.0％、財貨・サービスの輸入が同 4.8％となるだろう。代替調達の進展に
伴う原油輸入量の急増を主因として 輸入が高い伸
びとなり、輸出の伸びを上回ったことから、外需は
成長率の押し下げ要因となった。 
2026年 7-9月期の地域別輸出数量指数を季節調整
値（当研究所による試算値）でみると、米国向けが
前期比 0.9％（4-6月期：同 2.8％） 、EU向けが前期
比▲2.4％（4-6月期：同 0.3％） 、アジア向けが前期
比▲0.3％（4-6 月期：同▲0.8％） 、うち中国向けが

M
Update
Macronix International Co Ltd  | Asia Pacific
Pricing hike lifts margin further;
shifting from NOR toward
NAND
3Q earnings affected by capacity reallocation:
• 3Q revenue: NT$24.2bn, +27% Q/Q, +195% Y/Y, 9% below our estimate; we
cite to capacity transition from NOR to NAND.
• Gross margin: 70.5%, +6.1ppt Q/Q, +57.0ppt Y/Y, 2.7ppt above our estimate –
helped by ~NT$750mn inventory reversal, mostly associated with NOR..
• EPS: NT$5.52, +41% Q/Q, 13% below our estimate.

•  Employee compensation accruals led to a 24% Q/Q operating expense
increase.
Shifting focus to NAND: NOR revenue increased 21% Q/Q despite a 12% Q/Q
decline in bit shipments, consistent with pricing strength. Management attributed
lower NOR shipments to capacity reallocation toward NAND; NAND revenue rose
39% Q/Q, mainly from pricing.
The first phase of capacity expansion is scheduled to enter volume production in
2H27; the additional NT$15.8bn investment could contribute in 2028.

Incremental capacity will favor NAND, in particular 3D NAND-based eMMC (i.e., MLC
NAND), with no planned increase in NOR capacity. The company will gradually
withdraw from ROM product.

Morgan Stanley Research 3
Valuation Methodology and Risks
Macronix International Co Ltd (2337.TW)
Base case, P/B, in line with our Greater China memory coverage, due to the industry's cyclical
nature. We apply a target multiple of 2.1x to our 2027 BVPS estimate vs. its historical average
of 1.5x since 2017. We continue to believe the P/B target is justifiable, as the MLC and legacy
TLC NAND could be in greater shortage into 2H26, with under-supply easily reaching 40%. We

believe the only supplier that could fill the gap remains Macronix.
Risks to Upside
n Wider legacy NAND supply/demand gap
n NOR up-cycle, driven by limited supply expansion and/or stronger demand
n Faster growth for Switch hardware and core titles
Risks to Downside
n Less severe legacy NAND supply/demand gap
n NOR down-cycle, driven by faster supply expansion and/or demand weakness
n Slower growth for Switch hardware and core titles

ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

UMC (2303.TW)

UMC
Company description
United Microelectronics Corporation (UMC) is a leading global semiconductor
foundry company. It provides high-quality IC fabrication services, focusing on
logic and various specialty technologies to serve all major sectors of the
electronics industry. UMC's comprehensive IC processing technologies and
manufacturing solutions include Logic/Mixed-Signal, embedded High-
Voltage, embedded Non-Volatile-Memory, RFSOI, and BCD. Most of UMC's

12-inch and 8-inch fabs with core R&D are located in Taiwan.
Investment strategy
We rate UMC shares a Buy. Overall inventory levels in PC, smartphone, and
consumer electronics are currently healthy, and we see limited inventory
correction downside. While macro uncertainties may linger, we believe UMC’s
niche technology offerings will help it outperform its trailing-edge foundry
peers. We also expect transfer orders from TSMC and the AI-on-device trend

to drive stronger demand, and see better UTR recovery for UMC going
forward.
Valuation
Our target price for UMC of NT$125 is based on 18x 2027 /28E EPS, which is
higher than the midpoint of its 3-year forward PE. We believe our target
multiple is justified by our belief that UMC should benefit from the robust
demand in 22/28nm, TSMC's increasing order outflow opportunity, long-term
opportunity in advanced packaging and photonics and that its niche

technology offerings should help it outperform its trailing-edge foundry
peers. Our target price equates to 2027E/28E PB of 3.8x/3.6x.
Risks
Key downside risks to our target price include: 1) a longer-than-expected
demand recovery; 2) more competition from peers in 28nm; 3) worse-than-
expected developments from the company's China investment; 4) a
significant decline in smartphone end-demand; 5) poor contribution from
Novatek's iPhone OLED business; and 6) fiercer ASP competition from

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be

## Document 139: 261029_ms_vanguard-DG

Source PDF: all report pdf/261029_ms_vanguard-DG.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 16
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

M
Idea
VSMC could cause  high-single digit (%) dilution to 2027 GM – above many
investors’ 3-5% expectation: On Vanguard’s latest earnings call, management noted
a successful trial run of VisionPower Semiconductor Manufacturing Company, its JV
with NXP Semiconductors. It confirmed that interposers are on track for mass
production  in 1Q27. We see revenue upside from the ramp – but depreciation could
heavily pressure margins. Management previously guided that VSMC could break

even at 30kwpm, more likely in late 2028, in our view (vs. current 8-inch margin at
30-35%). VSMC  capacity is scheduled at 44kwpm, ~100kwpm 8-inch equivalent,
about one-third of current 8-inch capacity (280kwpm). On the other hand, we think
new capacity may only run at <5kwpm in 1Q27 and <20kwpm by end-2027;
depreciation reflects 44kwpm (VSMC's full capacity) for infrastructure, clean room,
and construction. We therefore project that VSMC's gross margin dilution in full-

year 2027 could be more severe than many expect.
Despite strong customer commitment, VSMC could still dilute the corporate
margin average for at least the next five years: Since management previously
guided that VSMC could break even at 30kwpm, we view its break-even point as late
2028 or 1H29. It will then take years for GM to rise from 0% to 30-40%, even if the
capacity is fully booked. We thus expect a drag on corporate average GM for at least

the next five years. The good news: VSMC demand is skewed to high-margin
businesses –  interposers, industrial, and automotive – so the margin improvement
could be smoother  vs. peers like Hua Hong.
2027 might bring double-digit wafer price hikes and AI-related revenue at 20-
30% of the mix: During its latest earnings call, UMC guided for a 10% blended
wafer ASP increase in 2027. Given larger exposure to AI server PMIC, industrial, and
automotive, we expect Vanguard to raise 8-inch wafer prices 10-15% for the full year.

Given some customer pushback to front-loaded hikes, the price hike might be split –
twice a year, each 5-10%. Also, the company would still have L TAs expiring in 2027,
so we don’t expect aggressive wafer ASP guidance for 1Q27. With new interposer
production, we believe AI-related revenue could reach 20-30% of total revenue.
Down to UW from EW: The margin impact is larger than we initially expected and
could last 5-10 years.  Our new price target implies 2.8x our 2027e BVPS.

Vanguard could still show strong revenue/margin
performance for 2H26
4Q26 should maintain strong growth: We expect Vanguard to sustain strong
performance into 4Q26, supported by resilient demand from the industrial and
automotive end markets. In addition to a favorable demand outlook, ongoing capacity
optimization initiatives, particularly the transition from thicker-wire to thinner-wire
manufacturing processes, should enhance production efficiency, improve capacity

utilization, and support higher output. These operational improvements, coupled with a
favorable product mix and steady order momentum, could further underpin revenue
growth and profitability in 4Q26. We now expect Vanguard 8-inch process margin to reach
34-36% in both 4Q26 and 1Q27.

Key questions to ask during the upcoming earnings call on November 6:
1.  Price hike into 2027: How much of the incremental cost could Vanguard pass
through  to customers? Could we see double-digit apples-to-apples price hikes into
2027 despite the expiration of some L TAs?

2.  AI-related revenue: How much could  AI related revenue grow in 2027 if we
include both AI server PMIC and interposer production?
3.  Singapore fab – margin dilution and capacity allocation: How could  margin be
affected by VSMC in both 2027 and 2028? How much would depreciation be in
2027 and 2028? How would the company allocate 12-inch capacity between
interposers, NXP, and other customers?
4.  Consumer demand: Has the company seen any slowdown in consumer demand?

And how soon could that capacity be filled up by other sources of demand?
5.  Further capacity expansion: Might the company have further plans to expand 8-
inch capacity or second Singapore fab since the utilization rate has been reaching
>90%?

6
Valuation  methodology
We lower our price target (base case value) to NT$135 from NT$180:  We factor in our
earnings estimate changes for 2027-28 and also lower the intermediate growth rate
assumption from 13% to 10.5% to reflect the long-term margin dilution.
We continue to derive our price target from a residual income model, which we believe
best captures the stock's long-term value. Our key assumptions other than the
intermediate growth rate are unchanged, including a cost of equity of 9.2% (beta of 1.2,

equity risk premium of 6% and risk-free rate of 2%),  and a terminal growth rate of 4.0%.
We also lower our bull and bear case values from NT$260 and NT$110 to NT$195 and NT
$80, accordingly, implying 4.0x and 1.7x our 2027e BVPS.

MS Rating
▪ We expect 2026 capex to increase beyond
2025's high level given Singapore fab

expansion, leading to long-term margin
pressure.
▪ We expect PMIC wafer price hikes in the
industry in response to strong demand from
AI. Vanguard could beneﬁt from TSMC
mature node consolidation as well as
interposer production.
▪ The new Singapore fab could cause high-
single-digit margin dilution to the corporate
average in 2027.
▪ VSMC's 12-inch capacity is all fully booked;
production will start to ramp in 2027 with
depreciation pressure during the early stage.

▪ Our UW rating is mostly because of the
margin pressure.

NT$195.00
4.0x base case 2027e BVPS
Healthy semi demand: Auto semi demand is
exceptionally strong; inventory is digested
quickly, and DDI for smartphones and large

panels shows robust growth. We forecast a
>30% revenue CAGR in 2025-28.
Competition from peers eases. We assume:
1) global semi sales growth of >20% and
foundry industry growth of >15% in 2026; 2)
better-than-expected demand for 8"
capacity; and 3) power demand for 5G
tracking ahead of schedule.
NT$135.00
2.8x base case 2027e BVPS
Semi growth slowdown: Demand has
started to pick up for LDDIC and PMIC,
while auto semis inventory digestion is
reaching an end. We forecast a 25% revenue

CAGR in 2025-28. Vanguard starts to
reverse pricing discounts to customers in
view of stronger demand and some rush
orders in the short run. We assume: 1) global
semi sales show growth in 2026; and 2) the
foundry industry shows single-digit growth
in 2026.
NT$80.00
1.7x base case 2027e BVPS
Semi growth decelerates: Auto semi demand
weakens markedly; power semi inventory
levels remain elevated. Smartphone DDI
shows a worse-than-expected decline.
Intensiﬁed competition and new Chinese

capacity dampen the overall pricing
environment. We forecast <10% revenue
CAGR in 2025-28. We assume: 1) overall
semi industry continues to decelerate in
2026 and foundry growth is stagnant; and 2)
demand for 8" capacity is weaker than
expected owing to faster product migration.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

公司研究報告 
本報告僅供宏遠投顧內部及客戶參考，雖已力求正確與完整，但因時間及市場客觀因素改變所造成產業、市場

1 
6239 力成(Powertech)

報告日期： 2026/10/28 研 究 員：翁浩軒  (Davy Wong)  
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 先進封裝比重提升，FOPLP 預期 2027 年放量貢獻 
 記憶體供需仍緊缺，稼動率維持高檔 
 訂單能見度高，預期明年營收獲利創高

一. 3Q26 毛利率優於預期，EPS 3.44 元 
力成 3Q26 營收 253.34 億(QoQ+9.6% 、YoY+26.87%) ，受惠於 DRAM
及 Logic 需求強勁，營收均季增約 15%，Nand 營收亦季增 5%。各
產品而言，DRAM 方面，訂單能見度高，推升營收穩定成長; Nand
方面，AI 伺服器帶旺 eSSD 需求，推升營收季增年增; Logic 業務
方面，出貨續增，營收季增年增雙位數; 超豐受到客戶需求強勁，
產品價格調整及簽訂 L T A 帶動營收季增年增雙位數;  T e r a  
Probe/TeraPower 營收持續季增年增雙位數。3Q26 毛利率受惠稼動
率提升而上升至 24.02%優於預期，唯營業費用較原先預期高，稅
後淨利 26.11 億(QoQ+17.71%、YoY+69.87%)，EPS 3.44 元。

二、4Q26 營收小幅季增，預估 EPS 4.18 元 
4Q26 預期記憶體、邏輯業務維持穩定成長，營收預估季增中低個
位數。DRAM 方面，記憶體產業仍處於供不應求加上客戶晶圓產
出極大化有利封測整體利用率，原材料成本上漲將適度反映至客
戶售價; NAND 方面，AI 帶動 SSD 及 Nand 封測需求維持高檔，且
隨封裝材料成本上升，力成將適度反映於封測價格上; Logic 業務
方面，受惠高階封裝 FCBGA 需求持續成長，整體產能利用率維持
高檔，FOPLP 客戶端產品驗證中，預期 2027 年量產。子公司 Tera 
Probe/Tera Power 受惠 HPC、AI、ADAS 及車用產品應用持續需求
的支撐，營收穩健成長，子公司超豐於 AI 與 HPC 帶動 Bumping
及 FC 封裝需求，菲律賓廠第四季起貢獻營收。預估力成 4Q26 營
收 262.21 億(QoQ+3.5%、YoY+22.49%)，毛利率 24.3%，稅後淨利
31.72 億(QoQ+21.46%、YoY+70.24%)，EPS 4.18 元。

三.  2026 / 2027 年獲利創高，預估 EPS 12.97 元/17.79 元 
展望 2026 年，AI 相關應用帶動公司 Memory 及 Logic 業務需求強
勁，預估今年逐季成長。Memory 業務方面，DRAM 及 Nand 封測
受惠於客戶拉貨積極，加上車用、網通、Server 等需求成長下，記
憶體位元出貨量穩定成長;隨著 AI 功能逐漸滲透到邊緣端及 AI 
投資評等：買進 
目標價位：320 元 
      18.0 X 2027 EPS 
參考價位：254.5 元 
     14.3X 2027 EPS

PC 、AI 手機，預期將有助於推升 Memory 需求。Logic 業務方面，
公司在 Flip Chip 、Bumping 等高階封裝產能增加，且在客戶滲透率
慢慢提高，搭配子公司超豐、Tera Probe/Tera Power 需求升溫，營
收持續成長。預估力成 2026 年營收 959.85 億(YoY+28.1%)，毛利
率 22.54%，稅後淨利 98.47 億(YoY+77.881%)，EPS 12.97 元。展望
2027 年 ， 記憶體供不應求延續 ， 加上 AI 帶動 logic 晶片封裝需求，
營收獲利持續創高，公司積極投入高階封裝業務，基於其記憶體
封裝薄晶片與晶片堆疊之技術開發新型態封裝產品，公司投入之
FOPLP 可整合高階 IC 功能提高效能，降低成本，2027 年將明顯貢
獻營收。預估力成 2027 年營收 1166.97 億(YoY+21.58%)，毛利率
23.57%，稅後淨利 135.05 億(YoY+37.14%)，EPS 17.79 元。

四.投資建議：買進，目標價 320 元 
投資建議方面，力成近年逐步從傳統記憶體封測跨入邏輯業務，
並拓展高階封裝，成為全方位封測廠。公司受惠於記憶體封測及
高階封裝需求，今明年營運正向，考量到 1)公司在先進封裝與國
際大廠合作，FOPLP、光通訊將成為新成長動能、2)明年記憶體供
需仍緊缺，稼動率維持高檔、3)今明年營收獲利創高，參考 2027
年 EPS 給予 18 倍本益比，目標價 320 元，建議買進。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介 
力成科技設立於 1997 年 5 月，專注於記憶體之封裝測試業務，為
全球第五大封測廠。公司聚焦記憶體產品，在大股東全球最大記
憶體模組製造廠商金士頓的支持下，與國際大廠策略結盟，如美
光、東芝、Intel、Sandisk 等，而取得量大且穩定訂單。公司記憶
體封測同業包括日月光、矽品、南茂、華東、福懋等公司。公司
2011 入股超豐，邏輯 IC 封測佔營收提高到 20％以上(主攻通訊市
場之高階邏輯封測)。2025 年營收比重分別為：封裝占比 67%，測
試占比 22%、SIP/Module 占 11%。從產品類別看，動態隨機存取記
憶體(DRAM)占比 22%，快閃記憶體(Flash)占比 25%，邏輯 IC 占比
42%、SIP/Module 占比 11%。

二.產業趨勢概況  
1. 力成持續擴充先進封裝佈局，FOPLP 將於 2027 放量 
力成為全球第一大記憶體封測大廠 ， 客戶為全球 DRAM 、 Nand Flash
大廠，近年公司積極拓展 logic 業務，營收比重已占四成以上，預
估未來邏輯業務將穩定貢獻營收，公司積極推進高階封裝佈局，
提供 2.5D、3D IC 封裝產品，其中 FOPLP 已取得客戶認證，公司
將以 FOPLP 提供 AMD AI 晶片，隨著機台陸續到位，預估 2027 年
上半年開始量產，因應客戶需求及訂單能見度提升，新購入 P12
廠區(購自友達)無塵室已建置完畢，將做 on substrate 的部分 ，而因
應 FOPLP 產品測試需求，子公司 TeraPower 亦新購置廠房，用於

### Page 4

Company Report 
公司研究報告

或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保
證。非經本公司同意，不得將本報告加以引用或轉載

4 
CP 、FT 測試，預估 2026~2027 年將建 400~500 台測試機台。另外，
公司亦與 Broadcom 在新加坡成立合資公司 ， 以力成在 FOPLP 封裝
經驗基礎下，共同開發及生產先進封裝基板上之加成式細線寬之
重佈層封裝技術，另外 Broadcom 亦帶領力成進入光通訊領域，預
期今年底小量生產 microbump Based OE 、 2027 下半年開始生產 CPO 
Swtich、2028 年量產 CPO AI 晶片。未來力成將跳脫傳統記憶體封
測廠，提供全方位封測服務。

2. AI 帶動 Logic 及 Memory 業務需求強勁 
AI 及雲端基礎建設投資強勁，持續支撐半導體及記憶體產業需
求，目前 DRAM 與 NAND 需求持續大於供給，產品價格趨勢有利
於記憶體產業營收成長 ， AI 相關應用將持續帶動公司成長 。 DRAM
方面，AI 強勁需求使整體記憶體仍然供不應求，整體產能利用率
可望維持高檔水準，並待客戶新晶圓產能陸續開出。隨著市場供
需持續緊俏，客戶對原物料成本上漲帶動的價格調整亦展現高接
受度。 Nand &SSD 方面， AI 應用推動企業級 SSD 及 NAND 相關需
求維持強勁，而封裝材料成本持續上升，力成將適度反映於客戶
價格，預期對營收及毛利率具正面效益。

Logic 業務方面， AI 市場持續增溫，力成在高階 FCBGA 同步受惠，
既有高階產品需求亦持續擴大，而先進封裝 FOPLP 方面，客戶端
產品驗證持續推進，預期 2027 年量產，此外隨著市場需求及量產
信心度提升，客戶在新產品導入 (NPI) 的需求亦顯著增加，力成除
持續推進 5 倍 reticle 的封裝應用外 ， 更進一步投入超大尺寸封裝技
術的研發，以因應未來 AI 晶片對高效能、高整合度的大尺寸封裝
需求。在高階 FCBGA 領域，第三季成功導入大晶片 (1x reticle Size)
封裝 (75x78) 的量產後，第四季將進一步跨入更大尺寸封裝領域
(78x88) ，整合 SoC Die 與 DRAM 元件在單一基板上。

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估

1、3Q26 毛利率優於預期，EPS 3.44 元 
力成 3Q26 營收 253.34 億(QoQ+9.6% 、YoY+26.87%) ，受惠於 DRAM
及 Logic 需求強勁，營收均季增 15%，Nand 營收亦季增 5%。毛利
率受惠稼動率提升而上升至 24.02%優於預期，唯營業費用較原先
預期高，稅後淨利 26.11 億(QoQ+17.71%、YoY+69.87%)，EPS 3.44
元。

2、4Q26 營收小幅季增，預估 EPS 4.18 元 
4Q26 預期記憶體、邏輯業務維持穩定成長，營收預估季增中低個
位數。預估力成 4Q26 營收 262.21 億(QoQ+3.5%、YoY+22.49%)，

## Document 179: 9月工业企业利润数据点评：利润增速反弹，价格驱动增强

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月工业企业利润数据点评：利润增速反弹，价格驱动增强.pdf
Report source: 联储证券
Date: 2026-10-28
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

证券研究报告 
宏观经济点评 2026 年 10 月 28 日

请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明

9 月工业企业利润数据点评：利润增速反弹，价格
驱动增强

[Table_Author] 
 魏争 分析师  沈夏宜 分析师

Email:weizheng@lczq.com Email:shenxiayi@lczq.com  
证书:S1320524100001 证书:S1320523020004

### Page 2

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 2 
6.7%，PPI 同比下降 2.0%；累计营业收入增长 2.6%，低于营业成本
3.8%的增速，利润同比下降 22.8%，降幅扩大 6.8 个百分点。二是酒饮等
部分消费行业利润边际改善，但需求修复仍不稳固。 酒、饮料和精制茶制
造业利润同比降幅由 34.7%明显收窄至 14.3%；医药制造业利润增速由
3.9%升至 4.4%；家具制造业利润降幅由 51.8%收窄至 51.3%；食品制造
业利润由增长 1.6%转为下降 0.5%。 
名义库存继续回升，实际补库动能并未同步增强，利润改善向经营现金流
的传导有待观察。从库存端看， 9 月末规上工业企业产成品存货同比增
长 11.6%，较 8 月提高 0.6 个百分点；按 PPI 粗略调整后的库存增速约为
6.6%，较 8 月回落 0.6 个百分点，指向名义库存加快增长部分受到价格上
涨影响，尚不能据此判断企业主动补库意愿增强。从经营效率看，库存及
回款周转效率边际改善，但经营资金占用压力仍存。9 月，产成品存货周
转天数由 21.3 天缩短至 21.0 天，应收账款平均回收期由 72.2 天缩短至
71.6 天；但应收账款同比增速由 9.0%升至 10.0%，企业负债同比增速由
7.0%升至 7.4%，指向周转效率改善尚未伴随应收账款及负债增长放缓，
利润改善能否进一步转化为现金流改善和主动扩张 仍有待观察。 
利润反弹主要受价格和利润率改善驱动，后续持续性仍取决于内需能否有
效接力。短期看，能源价格和 AI 产业链仍是工业利润的两条主要支撑，
但持续性存在差异。一方面，能源链盈利受国际大宗商品价格波动影响较
大；另一方面，AI 产业链盈利更多取决于订单和资本开支景气的延续。9
月 PPI 与工业利润同步反弹，但营收增速并未明显加快，指向盈利改善尚
未形成需求、收入与利润的良性循环。若后续上游价格持续上涨，而中下
游产品价格和订单未能同步改善，部分传统制造业仍将面临成本压力。 往
后看，重点关注三个信号，一是需求改善能否推动价格修复由上游向加工
工业及终端消费品扩散；二是新型政策性金融工具、 “六张网”等重大项
目能否加快形成实物工作量，带动传统工业品需求和收入修复； 三是应收
账款回收和库存周转效率能否持续改善，推动利润修复向经营现金流和主
动投资传导。

风险提示：国际大宗商品价格波动超预期；AI 产业链需求及外需景气回落
超预期；内需改善和政策落地进度不及预期。

投资评级说明 
投资建议的评级标准  评级 说明 
评级标准为报告发布日后的 6 个月内公司股价 （或行业
指数） 相对同期基准指数的相对市场表现。 其中A 股市
场以沪深 300 指数为基准； 新三板市场以三板成指 （针
对协议转让标的） 或三板做市指数 （针对做市转让标的）
为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准。 
股票评级 
买入 相对同期基准指数涨幅在 10%以上 
增持 相对同期基准指数涨幅在 5%~10%之间 
中性 相对同期基准指数涨幅在-5%~+5%之间 
减持 相对同期基准指数跌幅在 5%以上 
行业评级 
看好 相对表现优于市场 
中性 相对表现与市场持平 
看淡 相对表现弱于市场

## Document 223: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 3Q26 Review_ 악재 소멸HD현대중공업(329180) 3Q26 Review_ 악재 소멸3Q26 Review_ 악재 소멸 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 3Q26 Review_ 악재 소멸HD현대중공업(329180) 3Q26 Review_ 악재 소멸3Q26 Review_ 악재 소멸 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
악조건 속에서도 이 정도 
HD현대중공업의 2026년 3분기 영업실적은 매출액 5조 7,536억원(yoy +30.2%, qoq 
`-9.1%), 영업이익 8,992억원(yoy +61.3%, qoq -13.5%)으로 당사 추정치에 대체로 
부합하였다. 조업일수 감소와 원화 강세로 매출액이 감소했으며, 임단협 타결로 
인한 성과금 및 격려금이 당분기에 비용으로 반영되었다. 해양플랜트 부문에서는 
RUYA 프로젝트의 공사 진행률 확대와 TRION 프로젝트의 C/O(계약 변경) 
180억원이 추가로 반영되며 매출액이 전분기대비 0.7% 증가하였다. 엔진기계 
부문은 환율 하락을 판가 상승과 D/F(이중연료) 비중 증가로 상쇄하며 전분기와 
유사한 영업실적을 달성했다. 
2027년에도 지속될 수주 시점 효과 
3분기 대형선 야드의 수주 시점별 매출 비중은 2022년 4%, 2023년 45%, 2024년 
31%, 2025년 20% 수준으로 파악된다. 상대적으로 선가가 낮았던 2022~2023년 
물량이 여전히 매출의 절반 가량을 차지하고 있는 셈이다. 따라서 2027년 말까지도 
고선가 물량의 매출 비중 확대가 이어질 것으로 예상된다. 이는 LNG운반선 매출 
비중 감소에도 불구하고 수익성을 방어할 수 있는 핵심 요인으로 작용할 전망이다.   
투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 550,000원으로 하향 
투자의견은 매수로 유지하나, 목표주가는 550,000원으로 34.5% 하향한다. 환율 
가정을 기존 1,450원 수준에서 1,400원으로 조정함에 따라 목표주가 산정의 기준이 
되는 2027~2028년 실적추정치를 하향조정하였다. 또한 미국 네오 클라우드 
업체들의 자금 조달 여건 악화로 데이터센터 증설이 지연되고 있어, 발전용 엔진 
증설을 근거로 동사에 부여하였던 프리미엄을 제거하여 P/E 배수를 19.8배로 
하향조정했다. 그러나 본업인 상선 부문은 향후 고부가가치 가스선의 발주 강세를 
전망하기 때문에 투자의견은 매수로 유지한다.

당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

Is AI Useless?

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Is AI Useless?
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 26 Oct 2026
View in browser

I. AI's Toxic Financial Trifecta
Recent AI jitters have been fueled by a potentially toxic trifecta of financial
constraints: elevated borrowing costs, uncertain investment returns, and
concerns about whether sufficient capital exists to finance everyone's
ambitious AI spending plans. Consider the following:
(1) Elevated borrowing costs. The 10-year Treasury yield is hovering
near the upper end of its "old normal" range of 4.0%-5.0% (chart). That
puts it at the highest level since 2007. Overall, credit spreads (the extra

### Page 2

yield paid over equivalent US Treasuries, such as the 10-year note) have
not blown out uniformly across the board. Instead, the market has
bifurcated sharply between the pristine balance sheets of mega-cap tech
companies and the leveraged balance sheets of pure-play
infrastructure/data center borrowers.
Yields on AI debt have not broadly surged relative to Treasuries for the
dominant Big Tech issuers, whose balance sheets continue to be treated
virtually like "proxy sovereigns." However, the credit market is increasingly

imposing credit discipline, demanding significantly higher risk premiums
and spreads from the leveraged second- and third-tier infrastructure
borrowers that are building out the physical plumbing of AI. Increased
concerns about the credit quality of AI borrowers have been reflected in
widening credit default spreads (chart).

### Page 3

(2) Uncertain returns. Gartner estimates that AI data center operators
must generate $8.5 trillion in cumulative revenues through 2030 to cover
their investments and associated financing costs and still achieve a 12%
return on invested capital. Yet Gartner projects no more than $3.6 trillion in
attributable AI revenues over that period. In our view, if the numbers don't
add up, the pace of the AI buildout will slow. That wouldn’t be a calamity.
(3) Increasingly constrained capital. Meta's Q3 earnings report

intensified concerns about capital availability. Despite strong revenue
growth, the company reported negative free cash flow for the first time
since 2012 and announced new non-voting shares to facilitate fundraising,
warning of an "increasingly difficult fundraising environment." Today, Meta
unveiled plans to raise up to $95 billion through equity offerings.
Meanwhile, Broadcom CEO Hock Tan identified capital as potentially the
biggest constraint on AI development. BlackRock CEO Larry Fink

described competition for capital as the fiercest of his career, while Nvidia
CEO Jensen Huang observed that neocloud demand is "off the charts,"
but capital is scarce.
Today, Elon Musk warned that more than 200 specialized AI cloud
providers (i.e., neoclouds) are headed for a wave of "guppy-level death
and consolidation." Musk stated that debt markets have "almost entirely
shut off access to money for any company that doesn't have substantial
revenue independent of AI data centers." In recent weeks, the neocloud

providers’ stock prices have tumbled (chart).

### Page 4

On balance, hyperscalers’ stock prices have held up relatively well (chart).
II. Will AI Pay Off?
Demand for compute nevertheless remains extraordinary. Global
spending on cloud infrastructure services surged 43% y/y to $143 billion in
Q2, its fastest growth in eight years. But how sustainable is that demand
growth? Ultimately, the marginal economic benefits derived from AI must
justify the marginal cost of supplying the necessary compute.
We believe AI is already generating tangible economic benefits, even if

they have yet to become clearly visible in aggregate macroeconomic
statistics. Consider the following:
(1) Anthropic's S-1. Anthropic’s IPO filing today offered five revealing
takeaways: First, revenues soared from $1.2 billion in H1-2025 to $16.3
billion in H1-2026. Second, operating losses narrowed from $2.1 billion to
$693 million, with Q2 marking the company's first quarterly operating profit
of $974 million. Third, revenue per dollar of compute expense surged 5.2-

fold to $2.08 between Q1-2025 and Q2-2026. Fourth, Anthropic held
$131.7 billion in cash and short-term investments as of June, alongside
$3.9 billion in H1 operating cash flow. Fifth, its enterprise footprint
expanded rapidly, with more than 11,900 customers spending at least
$100,000 annually as of September, up from roughly 950 a year earlier.
(2) Corporate America's emerging AI payoffs. An August Janus
Henderson study of 109 companies found that 69% reported some margin

### Page 5

improvement associated with productivity gains and AI-related cost
reductions. Among the examples cited, Shell reported $400 million in
annual savings and a 45% reduction in unplanned downtime; Freeport-
McMoRan increased mill throughput 10% while cutting unit costs by up to
15%; inventories at one Hitachi facility fell 50% and lead times shortened
by 77%; and IBM reported $4.5 billion in AI-related savings in 2025.
Last Thursday, Barclays announced plans to eliminate approximately

5,000 jobs, or 5% of its workforce, as AI and broader technological
modernization improve efficiency. PayPal is targeting $1.5 billion in
annualized savings, partly through AI adoption.
(3) Corporate profit margins. Corporate America is becoming more
profitable. The forward profit margin of the S&P 500 excluding its
Information Technology sector has been climbing this year, reaching
12.9% in October (chart). Excluding just the Semiconductors industry, the

forward profit margin rose to a record 14.4%. The forward profit margin in
Semiconductors rose to a record 51.8% in the week of October 23. But
most of the industry’s margin expansion since 2024 is attributable to
capacity constraints pushing prices higher.
A closer look reveals encouraging trends in industries where AI
applications could be particularly beneficial. Forward profit margins have
expanded in numerous financial industries where AI offers significant

opportunities for automation (chart).

### Page 6

Health Care stands to benefit from AI applications that can streamline
clinical workflows and administrative processes (chart).
(4) Bottom line. We aren't yet seeing significant AI-driven profit margin
expansion across the economy, but anecdotal evidence of AI-related
productivity gains is mounting. We expect AI adoption to become
increasingly widespread across industries, eventually delivering
substantial productivity growth and boosting corporate profit margins. AI's

economic payoff may not yet be obvious in aggregate statistics, but that
doesn't mean it isn't already materializing.
💡

### Page 7

## Document 340: LatAm’s silicon dividend_ tailwinds from the AI investment boom in the U.S. (2026-10-28)

Source PDF: all report pdf/LatAm’s silicon dividend_ tailwinds from the AI  investment boom in the U.S. (2026-10-28).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-10-28
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

LatAm’s silicon dividend: 
tailwinds from the AI  
investment boom in the U.S.

1 
Authors 
Fancisco Campos 
Chief LatAm Economist

Beatriz Garcia Nunes 
LatAm Economist

October 28, 2026 
Key messages: 
- U.S. investment has broken away from Europe and China since 2023, 
powered by AI-related equipment, software and research spending. 
- The AI CapEx boom demands commodities and merchandise that LatAm 
can supply.  
- Latin America’s export gains are real but concentrated. Copper, computer 
and electronics products provide the clearest links to the U.S. AI buildout. 
- Chile is the region’s most direct copper beneficiary. Mexico captures the 
windfall stemming from increases hardware demand and trade-diversion. In 
these cases, the AI-related tailwinds have undergirded appreciable 
improvements in external accounts. 
- The cases for nickel and zinc illustrate that the dividend is not widespread. 
U.S. imports of the former have mostly returned toward pre-pandemic levels, 
while for the latter bilateral gains partly reflect supplier substitution. 
- While expanding AI use, supply shortages and incentives can extend the 
CapEx boom, its financial viability poses downside risks. 
Another ‘American exceptionalism’ story  
Investment has become one of the sharpest dividing lines among the world’s 
core economic blocs. Since 2024, U.S. gross fixed capital formation has firmed 
up appreciably. Meanwhile, investment in Europe has broadly stalled while in 
China’s it has retreated, the drivers of which are beyond the scope of this report 
(Figure 1).  
The U.S. divergence has largely been an AI story: a burst of spending on 
computing power, digital infrastructure and intellectual property has offset 
weakness in conventional construction and housing. Indeed, total U.S. fixed 
investment is nearly 12% above its 2023 level. Within it, investment in 
information-processing equipment has risen by roughly 57%, software outlays by 
about 35%, and research and development by around 15%. By contrast, non-
residential structures and residential investment sit below their 2023 levels 
(Figure 2).

### Page 2

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 2

Figure 1: The global investment divergence

Figure 2: AI-related categories lead U.S. investment

Source: US Bureau of Economic Analysis, Eurostat, National Bureau of Statistics of 
China, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Source: US Bureau of Economic Analysis, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Latin America’s commodity channel 
AI is not merely a semiconductor story. Chips require specialized minerals; data 
centers require electricity and cooling; electricity requires grids, transformers and 
storage. That chain pulls in copper, aluminum, silver, tin, lithium and selected 
semiconductor inputs, along with natural gas and uranium. Latin America has 
meaningful exposure to several of these inputs, but copper has emerged as the 
commodity with the cleanest and most economically significant regional link. 
Copper is used throughout the AI infrastructure stack, including power cables, 
substations, transformers, server boards, heat sinks and cooling systems. Rising 
U.S. copper demand has lifted imports from Chile and Peru, particularly the 
former (Figure 3). T he effect matters beyond merchandise export volumes. 
Favorable terms-of-trade have helped Chile generate a current -account surplus 
for the first time in roughly fifteen years (0.2% of GDP as of Q2 2026 on a 4 -
quarter rolling basis ) and have allowed Peru to log the largest surplus in about 
forty-five years (4.5% of GDP as of Q2 2026 on a 4-quarter rolling basis).

28 October 2026

### Page 3

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 3

Figure 3: Copper epitomized the commodity link

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Not every metal is copper 
The broader mining story demands more caution, though. U.S. nickel imports have 
recovered toward pre-Covid levels, but the upswing has not displayed the same 
persistence as copper. Mexico has captured a disproportionate share of the 
bilateral improvement, yet that gain looks more like normalization than a 
sustained AI -driven break (Figure 4). Zinc tells a similar story. U.S. real imports 
stand shy of their pre-pandemic levels, and stronger purchases from Mexico and 
Peru partly reflect a reallocation away from Brazil rather than a decisive increase 
in U.S. demand (Figure 5).

Figure 4: Nickel: recovery, not a boom

Figure 5: Zinc: a substitution story

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

28 October 2026

### Page 4

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 4

Mexico’s hardware advantage 
The larger non-commodity prize is in computers and electronics. U.S. imports of 
these products have sky -rocketed, while the supplier mix has shifted away from 
China. Mexico and various Asian economies have gained share on the back of this 
double-whammy impulse (Figure 6). 
This helps explain why Mexico’s non-automotive manufacturing exports, notably 
electronics, have outpaced automotive shipments to the U.S. and pressures from 
higher oil prices. The resulting non -oil trade surplus has offset pressure from the 
oil balance and helped Mexico generate a balanced current account (0% of GDP 
as of Q2 2026 on a 4 -quarter rolling basis). The external boost is meaningful, 
although it sho uld not necessarily be interpreted as bona fide  evidence of 
nearshoring, in our view.

Figure 6: U.S. computer & electronics imports going into overdrive

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research.

How much runway remains? 
Quantifying or mapping out a timeframe for the AI investment outlook is beyond 
the scope of this piece or our expertise. Nevertheless, we’d venture to argue that 
the capex cycle appears more likely to mature gradually than to stop abruptly . 
One reason is that the investment case is shifting from frontier -model training 
toward enterprise inference . Capacity that once served development can 
increasingly run commercial workloads for companies. That creates a path to 
revenue even if artificial general intelligence (AGI) crystallizes later than 
expected. It also spreads demand to a much larger corporate customer base.  
A second reason is scarcity. Graphics processing units (GPUs) remain tight, while 
power availability, grid connections, cooling equipment, construction capacity 
and data -center complexity limit how quickly supply can respond. Bottlenecks 
may therefore exte nd the cycle. The constraint is especially relevant for copper 
28 October 2026

### Page 5

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 5

and power infrastructure, where projects have long lead times and permitting 
hurdles. 
A third reason is strategic. AI development resembles a prisoner’s dilemma at 
both the corporate and national levels. Each participant may worry about costs, 
safety and control, yet slowing unilaterally risks surrendering technological, 
commercial or natio nal security advantages. The same tension is visible in calls 
for guardrails alongside continued spending. Competition can therefore sustain 
capex even when short-term returns are uncertain. 
We acknowledge the risks, nonetheless. Higher borrowing costs raise the hurdle 
rate for an infrastructure buildout financed partly through debt. More important, 
revenues must eventually justify the installed capital base. The bearish case is not 
that AI la cks value; it is that infrastructure spending may run ahead of 
monetization, compromising some companies’ repayment capacity. Public 
opposition to the development of AI infrastructure and/or adoption of AI 
technologies could also slow down the technology’s roll-out. 
The regional balance  
Summing up, the AI investment boom in the U .S. is delivering a dividend to parts 
of Latin America, not the region as a whole. Chile and Peru benefit through copper 
prices and export volumes. Mexico benefits through electronics manufacturing 
and the replacement of Chinese supply. For Brazil and Colombia, the AI dividend 
is rather muted. Where materialized, furthermore, the dividend supports trade 
and current accounts more clearly rather than improving the domestic 
investment. 
These dynamics should persist while U.S. AI investment remains supply -
constrained and strategically protected. If widespread adoption advances and 
the strategic race intensifies, the impulse can last. If financing costs rise further 
or monetization disappo ints, the cycle may narrow. Either way, copper and 
Mexican electronics remain the region’s most visible gauges of the U.S. AI 
investment appetite.

28 October 2026

### Page 6

Deutsche Bank Research Institute 
LatAm’s silicon dividend: tailwinds from the AI investment boom in the U.S. – October 28, 2026

The New Zealand economy has returned to growth over
the last year, despite a number of headwinds. That’s been
accompanied by some modest growth in jobs, though
to date it hasn’t been enough to prevent a further rise

in the unemployment rate. However, we think that next
Wednesday’s labour market reports could show the jobs
market reaching a turning point.
We expect the unemployment rate to ease from 5.6%
to 5.5% in the September quarter, which would mark
the first fall in almost five years. That’s not a firmly held
view, given the usual margin of uncertainty around this

measure. But we’re now seeing consistent evidence
that jobs growth is outstripping the modest pace of
population growth. We also note that the previous result
was 5.56% in unrounded terms, so it wouldn’t take much
to tip the rounded rate down to 5.5% this time.
On the whole, our forecasts are a little stronger than
what the RBNZ assumed in its September Monetary
Policy Statement. While our unemployment rate forecast

is identical, we’re more positive on both employment
and labour force participation. Meanwhile, our pick of a
0.5% rise in the Labour Cost Index is slightly ahead of the
RBNZ’s estimate of 0.4%.
Assuming that the RBNZ delivers the OCR hike that we
expect this afternoon, we think they will be noncommittal
about the chances of another hike at the 9 December
Monetary Policy Statement. In that respect, the labour
market surveys could be important in setting market

expectations for the December review, as there aren’t
that many major data releases between now and then.
Forecast details.
We expect the Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS)
to show a 0.5% rise in employment for the September
quarter. That’s broadly in line with the Monthly
Employment Indicator (MEI), with the September release
this morning showing a third straight month of growth
in filled jobs. We note that employment growth in the
HLFS has actually outstripped the MEI for the last nine

quarters, but it’s not clear why this has been the case and
we’re wary of extrapolating it too far into the next quarter.

Employment growth of around 0.5% would be more than
enough to absorb the modest 0.3% rise in the working-
age population, and on its own would suggest a fall in

the unemployment rate. But it also comes down to the
rate of labour force participation, which we don’t really
have much guidance on. The participation rate has been
choppy over recent quarters, and in the June quarter
we saw an unusually large 0.3pt rise to 70.7%; we’re
assuming some payback this time with a more modest
0.1pt rise to 70.8%.
We’ll also be keeping an eye on the broader indicators of
slack in the labour market, although we don’t formally use

these in our forecasts. The June quarter saw a sharp rise
in the underutilisation rate from 12.9% to 13.8%. However,
we think there’s some residual seasonality in this series,
relating to one component in particular: people who are
available for work but not actively looking. In that case,
we could see a substantial pullback in the underutilisation
measure this time.
Previously we’ve characterised the labour market as a
“low-fire, low-hire” environment, with much of the rise in

unemployment reflecting the difficulty of breaking into
the workforce, rather than job losses. There are some
signs of a thaw emerging, with job vacancies picking up
from their lows over the last year and business surveys
pointing to a lift in hiring intentions. But we still expect
that it will be a long road to recovery – our current
forecasts see the unemployment rate at 5% by the end of
next year.
While cost-of-living pressures are again rearing their

head as a major concern for workers, the existing degree
of slack in the labour market will continue to make it
difficult for them to push for bigger pay increases. We
expect a 0.5% rise in the Labour Cost Index (LCI) for
the September quarter, which would nudge the annual
growth rate up slightly to 2.1%. That said, we don’t have a
lot to go on for this forecast. The MEI includes a measure
of median weekly earnings, which has shown no sign of
acceleration so far – but this is only a very loose indicator

of wage pressures, as it will also reflect any changes in
the composition of jobs and pay rates.

## Document 357: Open mouth operations (2026-10-31)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Open mouth operations (2026-10-31).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#australia #luciellis #rba
Open mouth operations
By Luci Ellis
Chief Economist Westpac Group, Westpac
13:34 October 23 2026 Share
The RBA does not want to promise what it will do, but it is increasingly trying to tell us what
it thinks.
Central banks’ communication is carefully scrutinised for every nuance. Lately, there seems to have
been more RBA communication to pore over. The method of delivery has also evolved. This has

### Page 2

Market participants pore over every utterance by central banks for clues to future rate moves. They will
have plenty to pore over in the run-up to the November RBA decision. After the flurry of fireside chats
this week, ’60 Minutes’ will run a pre-recorded interview with chief economist Sarah Hunter on Sunday.
Next Wednesday, the whole senior policy team will appear before Senate Estimates, just as the
quarterly CPI is released. (This timing will have been the Senate’s choice, not the RBA’s.) After the

decision will come the Statement on Monetary Policy, a media release and press conference. More
speeches will follow, including one by the Deputy Governor on the Thursday.
RBA communication does seem to have changed, not just following the RBA Review but also more
recently. There may be fewer policy meetings each year, but the communication outside that certainly
feels more frequent than a couple of years ago. Back in 2024, speaking engagements felt a little

sparse outside the post-meeting media conference and parliamentary appearances. We have counted
58 public engagements so far this year on the Speeches page on the RBA’s website. This compares
with 58 for the whole of last year and 52 in 2024. Excluding the ‘compulsory’ press conferences and
parliamentary appearances, fewer than half of the 2024 events were related to economics, markets
and monetary policy.
The ‘how’ has changed
The way the RBA communicates with markets has changed as well. There are fewer set-piece

speeches on policy and a lot more semi-scripted ‘fireside chats’. Compared with a few years ago, there
are more interviews across a wide range of media. This is a big change from the pre-Review world. It
was a huge deal when the previous Governor appeared on the 7:30 Report; it is hard to imagine him
agreeing to an Assistant Governor appearing on 60 Minutes.
Part of the change reflects increased efforts to reach a general audience. Central banks want to explain

why they do what they do. This matters especially when interest rates are rising but the household
sector is not the source of the inflation.
Speaking differently to different audiences can sometimes confuse markets. As the RBA’s Chief
Communication Officer highlighted in a recent panel discussion, the post-meeting media conference is
designed to speak to the public. Market participants and economists certainly tune in, but we are not
sometimes caused confusion when tone seems to vary. Different audiences call for different tone and

varying complexity.
The content has evolved, too. RBA officials are increasingly using speeches directed to professional
audiences to discuss more technical estimates of inferred variables such as neutral rates and
financial conditions. They are also highlighting that monetary policy combats inflation in more ways
than just squeezing mortgage holders.
One thing has not changed: the RBA is not in the business of providing ‘forward guidance’ promises

about the future path of interest rates. It does, however, give more running commentary about how it
is interpreting recent data. These interpretations are themselves useful signals about what the RBA
thinks it might do.

### Page 3

the primary audience. So if you think that what the Governor said in the media conference sounded
more ‘dovish’ than what another official says in a more market-oriented forum, it is partly because she
is trying to convey that the RBA understands that its recent policy actions are making things harder for
many Australians. But this is in much the same way that a doctor might say, “This might hurt a bit.” It
does, but that will not stop the treatment.

A related issue is that it is easier to control tone in pre-drafted material such as the minutes and set-
piece speeches than in unstructured Q&A. Senior central bankers are trained to be careful with their
words in unscripted settings. If the tone differs, the minutes are still more likely to say what the Board
really means than an off-the-cuff answer.
Greater communication with the broader public might have reduced the pressure to make the set-piece

speeches more accessible. Research on central bank communication often uses measures such as
the Flesch–Kincaid Grade Level and Flesch Reading Ease score. A comprehensive analysis was
beyond the scope of this note. However, a spot comparison suggests four speeches from 2026 (1, 2, 3,
4) were harder to read than four from 2021 (1, 2, 3, 4). The 2021 speeches scored in the 11 to 12
range, which is high-school-level complexity. The 2026 speeches scored between 12 and 15, which is

academic or technically dense text requiring university-level education. (Including two of my own 2021
speeches is an obvious caveat, but the two speeches by then-Governor Phil Lowe also scored better
on these metrics than the sample from this year. It should be noted, though, that readability metrics do
not necessarily imply better clarity if the concepts discussed are more abstract.)
The ‘what’ has changed
As noted above, the content of RBA communication has evolved, too. As its intellectual framework has

begun to put more weight on the balance of supply and demand, and those invisible ‘star’ variables
(NAIRUs, neutral real rates, ‘financial conditions’, etc.), the communication to professional audiences
has increasingly focused on those topics.
More recently, the RBA has also placed more emphasis on the full range of transmission channels from
interest rates to inflation (though not, as we have noted previously, the labour supply channel). This is

understandable in a world of supply shocks. People need to understand that monetary policy is not
simply an exercise in hitting mortgage borrowers. We doubt they would be comforted, though, by the
Deputy Governor’s explanation this week that the ‘cashflow channel’ was the smallest of the
transmission channels from interest rates to inflation, with the exchange rate much more prominent.
That implies a lot of pain for a small group, for little effect on inflation. It raises the question of whether

other policy tools or institutional arrangements might get the same effect on inflation through the other
channels without hitting a minority of households disproportionately.
We have also seen a move away from using catchphrases to summarise the position. From ‘narrow
path’ to ‘not ruling anything in or out’ to ‘cautious and predictable’, once entrenched, a phrase can
outlast its usefulness.

### Page 4

Related articles
One thing that has not changed is the way the minutes record the presentations to the Board and the
members’ discussion. The minutes rarely convey much new information beyond the post-meeting
statements. Sometimes, though, the minutes reveal the Board’s reaction to the staff’s analysis. The
August 2024 minutes were a good example: the discussion highlighted just how sensitive the forecasts
were to key judgements by the staff.
The ‘why’ has not changed

Despite these changes, the underlying objectives of the RBA’s communication have not changed. It
wants to explain – and perhaps justify – its decisions. And it wants to describe how it interprets
incoming data, especially inflation, the labour market and the balance of supply and demand.
The RBA, like any central bank, is not really in the business of providing ‘forward guidance’ about
future moves. Circumstances change, so it can never guarantee a particular future path for interest

rates. Some academic literature argues that promising to keep rates low for a long time (or high, for
that matter) independently amplifies the transmission of monetary policy beyond the effect of current-
period interest rates alone, by shifting the short end of the yield curve. Central banks including the RBA
have certainly tried jawboning in this way from time to time. The prevailing view, though, is that the
potential cost of reputational loss if things do not turn out as predicted outweighs the benefits of a

slightly stronger transmission mechanism.
In this context, it is the interpretation of data that provides the signal about future policy directions. This
is why it was noteworthy that the Governor still characterised the labour market as ‘a little tight’ after the
September meeting, even though the Board’s post-meeting communication did not. We have been
noting for some time that the RBA staff have a downbeat view of supply capacity. Together with the

RBA’s oft-stated concerns about inflation, this is why the Deputy Governor’s ‘hawkishness’ did not
surprise us.

2026 TPCA ： AI 帶動 PCB 全 鏈 規 格 升 級 
AI 規 格 升 級 延 續 ， 材 料 供 給 偏 緊 支 撐 漲 價 
⚫ 高速材料 朝 M9/M10 推進，石英布 需求明確  
⚫ 玻纖布及銅 箔供給偏緊， CCL 漲價延 續至 2027 年 
⚫ NPO 為光通訊 過渡主流， PCB 層數與製 程同步升級  
台光電 (2383) ：公司為 M8 世代首家量產之銅箔基板 (CCL) 供應商，主
要應用於 1.6T 交換器及 AI 伺服器， 2026 年 M9(EM -896) 亦為首家量
產 ， 其中EM-8962 鎖定 ASIC 應用 ， 預計1Q27 量產 。 規格方面 ，EM-892
系列以上屬 M8 等級， K 代表搭配低介電 (Low Dk) 玻纖布，交換器採
用 K2，UBB 因板面積較大改採 K3；K3 及 K4 皆為石英布方案， K4
的 Dk/Df 較前代改善約 50% ，898K4 目前送樣認證中，預計 1Q27 提
供樣品。 M10 送樣中，預計 2026 年完成認證、 2027 年進入 NPI，M10
以 pure 為主 ，M8/M9 則以 hybrid 為主，以兼顧成本與剛性。 PTFE 雖
電性佳，但缺乏玻布支撐，良率與電鍍仍待改善，公 司 不 發 展 PTFE ，
聚焦於既有 HDI 架構下之材料開發。  
載板材料方面， EM-8963 具低熱膨脹係數 (CTE) 特性，已達載板水 準 ，
先導入光模組應用。目前載板材料市場以日韓廠為主，但日韓廠有技
術、缺產能，公司憑藉 HDI 基礎，具良率控制優勢，預期切入速度快。
CPO 方面，公司認為短期 NPO 仍為主流，客戶不會採用缺乏信用紀
錄的材料，公司兼具客戶信任與量產良率； 3.2T scale out 亦有機會採
用 M10。產能方面，公司目前月產能約 600 萬張，預計 2–3 年內翻倍，
2027 年新增之 315 萬張產能全數為 M8 以上高階產品，有助於提高高
階材料占比。

德宏 (5475) ： 公司主要產品為石英纖維紗 (Q 紗)及石英纖維布 (Q 布)，
台灣廠目前僅生產 Q 紗，蘇州廠則同時具備 Q 紗及 Q 布產線。台灣 Q
布擴產方面，織布機將於 4Q26 起陸續裝機，預計 3Q27 台灣開始生產
Q 布，初期產能與大陸廠相當，後續依市場狀況逐步增加織布機台數。
織布機自下單到交機需 2–3 年，設備交期為擴產的主要限制。原料方
面，石英純度高 ，原料不會缺貨，一根石英棒可拉出 2–8 萬米纖維。
技 術 方 面 ， 公司 石 英 纖維 專 利 為 自 有， 技 術 路線 與 台 玻(1802) 不同，
拉紗製程需長期累積經驗。另外，因 E 布價格上漲，公司已在中國廠
恢復 E 布生產，因設備仍保留，可較快復產並受惠於 E 布漲價。

光通訊方面，公司展出 800G、1.6T、XPO 及 NPO 模組板，800G 採 10–12 層，1.6T
及 XPO 採 12–16 層， NPO 達 14–18 層，並導入 mSAP 、埋孔與 Cavity 製程，最
細線寬線距達 25/30μm ，傳輸速率每升一世代，層數及製程精細度皆同步提升。
技術路線方面， CPO 目前主要由輝達 (NVIDIA) 推動，但終端客戶不希望供應鏈集
中於單一供應商， NPO 因此成為折衷方案，並帶動 SLP 類載板需求增加；公司認
為目前真正具量產趨勢的是 NPO ，各技術之間並非互相取代，最終取決於應用場
景。架構方面， 2026 年 CPO 主要應用於 scale out ，scale up 目前仍以可插拔方案
為主，考量功耗與材質，公司預期未來 scale up 用量將達 scale out 的 10 倍以上。

用於 GPU 大廠加速器 ， 預計 1H27 放量 。 產品結構方面 ，2026 年以 M4/M6 為主，

825 萬張、 2029 年底 915 萬張，擴產以無錫廠及泰國廠為主。

目前公司稼動率約 75%，主要受玻纖布短缺限制，缺料程度依序為 E-glass、Low 
Dk2 、Low Dk1 ，原預期 2H27 舒緩，現看來可能一路偏緊至 2028 年，待陸廠玻
纖布產能開出後，稼動率改善將加速財務表現。漲價方面， 3Q26 漲幅超過 15% ，
之後每季以此幅度為基準持續調漲至 2Q27 ，漲價效應將延續至 2027 年中至下
半年； HVLP4/5 隨需求放大亦將出現短缺，銅箔廠 10 月已開始調價，公司將持
續轉嫁成本。我們認為公司高階產品於 2027 年陸續放量，加上漲價效應延續及
產能持續擴充，營運動能可望延續，投資建議買進，目標價 945 元(2027 年 EPS 
× 18 倍 PER) 。

年可做到 22 層搭配 mSAP 之 3.2T 產品。網通與資料中心營收於 2Q26 及 3Q26
皆已占集團總營收 10% ，預期 4Q26 光模組明顯增量， 2027 年隨新產能逐季開
出，光模組營收規模將快速成長，網通與資料中心營收規模有機會超越衛星產
品。技術路線方面， CPO 屬載板範疇，公司未投入，並認為 CPO 仍有成本等問
題，過渡期間方案競爭仍多。 AI 加速卡方面，隨運算能力提升，板子層數及階
數同步增加，公司目前可做到 44 層 9 階 HDI ，後續將推進至 56 層 10 階。原料
方面， CCL 供應仍偏緊，漲價可能持續，但隨替代料源陸續獲終端客戶採用，
供應彈性已逐步增加。整體來說，我們正面看待公司後續營運狀況。

## Document 418: US Equity Views_ Over-earning, but not an earnings bubble

Source PDF: all report pdf/US Equity Views_ Over-earning, but not an earnings bubble.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-09-17
Pages: 18
Extraction status: ok

### Page 2

semiconductor share prices have recently lagged the trajectory of near-term EPS
estimates. Similarly, the market trades at a P/E discount relative to what a macro model
would imply given the current level of corporate profitability. Our 12-month S&P 500
return forecast of +14% (to 8,700) reflects the view that earnings growth, rather than
expanding valuations, will remain the primary driver of the bull market.
17 September 2026   2
Goldman Sachs US Equity Views

### Page 3

The magnitude of S&P 500 “over-earning”

S&P 500 earnings grew by an extraordinary 51% year/year in Q2 2026 and have 
grown by 26% during the past four quarters. During the last 30 years, the recent pace 
of earnings growth has been exceeded only during the post-recession rebounds in 2010 
and 2021.

The recent strength of S&P 500 earnings growth has raised investor concerns that 
current earnings power is unsustainable and that the market is in an “earnings 
bubble.” S&P 500 earnings have recently outpaced both their historical trend and their 
historical relationship with US GDP growth. S&P 500 4-quarter EPS growth has averaged 
7% during the past 30 years, and the relationship between S&P 500 earnings and US 
economic growth in recent decades would have pointed to a similar rate of earnings 
growth during the last few quarters.

Exhibit 1: S&P 500 trailing 4-quarter EPS has grown by 26% during the past year

### Page 4

The degree to which the US equity market is currently “expensive” depends in large 
part on the sustainability of recent earnings strength. The exceptional recent 
strength of earnings has driven an unusual wedge between equity valuation multiples 
based on near-term earnings and valuations on trend earnings. Because equity prices 
have failed to keep pace with surging earnings, near-term valuations show no hint of a 
bubble: The S&P 500 12-month P/E ratio has declined from 23x a year ago to 19x today 
and now matches its 10-year average. However, even an “average” multiple may be 
expensive if current earnings are unsustainable. The cyclically-adjusted P/E based on 
trailing 10-year earnings has climbed to one of its highest levels on record, falling shy of 
the highs of 1999-2000 but surpassing the level reached in 2021. Similarly, our 
colleagues have argued that the increase in market value of AI companies during the last 
few years requires an optimistic estimate of the future magnitude of AI-related profits 
and who will capture them.

### Page 5

The AI boom and S&P 500 earnings growth

Affirming investor concerns that US companies are “over-earning,” there are three 
factors that are boosting earnings today but will contribute to deceleration going 
forward: 1) AI capex spending, 2) semiconductor margin expansion, and 3) earnings 
from private investment gains.

AI capex 
The AI investment boom is driving nearly half of S&P 500 EPS growth this year, but 
its contribution will fade going forward. The mega-cap US hyperscalers are on track to 
spend $800 billion on capex this year, an increase of 94% vs. 2025. That spending is 
flowing through to the earnings of the AI infrastructure complex including 
semiconductor, tech hardware, industrials, and utilities companies, which are collectively 
accounting for roughly half of consensus S&P 500 earnings growth this year. The AI 
boom is also having a secondary impact on earnings outside of the infrastructure 
complex, for example by boosting capital markets activity and consumer wealth. Both 
consensus and GS analyst forecasts show hyperscaler capex growing at a slower rate in 
coming years, which should result in decelerating earnings growth for much of the AI 
infrastructure complex and a fading tailwind to S&P 500 earnings.

### Page 6

Hyperscaler depreciation expenses will continue to increase as capex growth 
decelerates, further dampening the boost of AI investment spending to S&P 500 
earnings growth. We estimate a drag from hyperscaler depreciation expenses on S&P 
500 earnings growth of 5pp in 2027, offsetting nearly half of the 11pp boost to earnings 
from capex spending. By 2028, the drag from depreciation should offset the S&P 500 
earnings uplift from continued capex spending.

### Page 7

If capex were fully expensed, consensus 2027 S&P 500 EPS would be 22% lower than 
under current accounting rules. The current gap between actual reported and 
hypothetical fully-expensed earnings is one of the widest in recent decades, exceeded 
only as earnings fell more quickly than capex during the recessions of 2001 and 2008.

Hyperscaler capex has consistently surprised relative to consensus estimates during 
the last few years, and the potential for additional surprises going forward creates a 
wide range of potential S&P 500 earnings outcomes. Our equity analysts expect 
hyperscaler capex will total $1.2 trillion in 2027 and $1.4 trillion in 2028, in which case 
the current tailwind from AI capex to S&P 500 EPS will fade by 2028. Exhibit 10 below 
outlines upside and downside scenarios around that base case. Next year, a $250 billion 
surprise in hyperscaler capex — in either direction — would shift S&P 500 EPS growth by 
about 6 pp in the same direction.  
We estimate that hyperscaler capex would need to decline by roughly 30%, to $570 
billion, to offset earnings growth for the rest of the S&P 500 in 2027. This decline 
would reduce earnings for the AI infrastructure complex by about 40%, leaving their 
profits about 25% above 2025 levels.

### Page 8

Semiconductor margin expansion 
A sharp increase in profit margins for many semiconductor companies has 
contributed to the above-trend recent pace of S&P 500 earnings growth. The 
combination of strong demand and limited supply has boosted semiconductor prices 
and gross margins, with the most pronounced margin expansion among memory stocks. 
S&P 500 memory firms currently enjoy gross margins of roughly 80%, more than double 
their historical average. Across the semiconductor industry, gross margins sit at their 
highest level in decades, and consensus expects margins to remain at similar levels 
through 2028.

Margin expansion has accounted for a large share of recent semiconductor earnings 
growth, but that boost should fade going forward. We estimate that about 1/4 of S&P 
500 Semiconductor earnings growth in 2026 is being driven by gross margin expansion. 
Our equity analysts believe the balance of memory supply and demand is likely to 
remain tight through 2027, with incremental supply additions unlikely to impact industry 
margins until 2028 at the earliest. However, they expect the rate of incremental margin 
expansion to slow meaningfully next year, reducing the contribution of semiconductors

S&P 500 earnings growth in the next few years will be highly sensitive to the 
trajectory of semiconductor margins. In a scenario where slowing AI infrastructure 
investment, increasing supply, and/or technological shift lowers semiconductor prices 
and profit margins, S&P 500 EPS growth would also disappoint. We estimate that each 
percentage point change in S&P 500 semiconductor gross margins next year would shift 
S&P 500 EPS growth by about 1 pp.

### Page 10

“Other income” from private investment gains 
Appreciating equity investment stakes are also temporarily inflating S&P 500 
earnings. Mega-cap tech earnings have recently been boosted by large GAAP “other 
income” related to their equity investments. In Q2 2026, the mega-cap tech companies 
enjoyed unrealized investment gains in private companies that totaled roughly $150 
billion, translating into 12% of S&P 500 EPS. We expect H2 2026 reports will include 
additional “other income” that should be larger than the gains in Q1 but smaller than Q2 
totals. We expect a much smaller contribution in 2027. The complete removal of this 
“other income” next year would create a drag of 8 pp on S&P 500 earnings growth in 
2027 relative to 2026, all else equal.

### Page 11

S&P 500 earnings outlook

Our baseline forecast is for S&P 500 EPS to rise to $415 in 2027 (+11% year/year) 
and to $460 in 2028 (+11%). We expect an above-consensus base of earnings in 2026 
due to further “other income” gains from private equity stakes in H2 of this year. 
Excluding the “other income” inflating earnings in 2026, S&P 500 earnings growth next 
year would register 18%. Our 2027 EPS forecast is similar to the bottom-up consensus 
estimate of $419 but above the median top-down strategist estimate of $403.

### Page 12

Our 2028 EPS forecast is about 5% below the bottom-up consensus, signaling a 
return to the typical historical pattern of downward revisions to consensus earnings 
estimates after the last two years of rising estimates. Outside of the complex of AI 
capex beneficiaries, we expect sales growth will register close to nominal GDP , margins 
will expand modestly, and a growing boost from AI productivity will result in earnings 
growth of 9% in both 2027 and 2028.  Our forecasts for EPS growth among this group of 
companies are roughly 4-5% below consensus estimates in both 2027 and 2028, similar 
to the historical average magnitude of downward revision to consensus estimates. While 
AI investment spending has had a direct impact on the earnings of semiconductor and 
other AI infrastructure stocks, it has also lifted earnings for companies outside of the “ AI 
complex,” including by boosting capital markets activity as well as supporting consumer 
spending via the wealth effect. 
Solid GDP growth and easing pressure from energy prices will be the main macro 
tailwinds for earnings growth in 2027. Economic growth is the primary variable in our 
top-down earnings model and explains more than half of the historical variation in S&P 
500 EPS growth. Our economists expect US real GDP growth will average 2.1% in 2027 
and 2.3% in 2028. Our commodity strategists expect Brent crude oil will decline to 
$80/bbl by the end of 2027, which would weigh modestly on Energy sector profits but 
boost earnings for most other sectors. Outside of the AI-related risks outlined above, 
persistent input cost pressures and risks to the consumer from equity market volatility 
are two of the key macro downside risks to our earnings forecast.

### Page 13

As the earnings tailwind from AI investment spending fades, the trajectory of S&P 
500 profitability in coming years will depend increasingly on the realization of AI 
productivity gains. Our forecasts embed a 1 pp boost to S&P 500 EPS growth from AI 
productivity in 2027 and a 2 pp boost in 2028. Economy-wide surveys and corporate 
commentary signal enterprise adoption that is increasing but remains in early stages, 
and the impact of AI adoption on corporate earnings still appears narrow. However, the 
recent acceleration in enterprise AI spending suggests that the earnings impact of AI 
adoption should become clearer in coming quarters.

### Page 14

Valuing S&P 500 earnings

Like our forecasts and consensus estimates, market pricing suggests investors 
anticipate a deceleration in earnings growth going forward. This is true both for the 
broad market and for AI infrastructure stocks. For example, the share prices of 
semiconductor stocks have historically mirrored the path of consensus EPS estimates, 
and the recent moderation of semiconductor returns has aligned with consensus 
estimates for decelerating growth in 2027 and 2028.

The current S&P 500 P/E multiple signals an optimistic outlook but market 
skepticism regarding the sustainability of current profits. In addition to rising interest 
rates, an anticipated deceleration in earnings helps explain the recent decline in the S&P 
500 forward P/E multiple. Along with macro factors like interest rates, the level of 
corporate profitability has helped explain the variation in S&P 500 valuation over time. 
Based on the historical relationship between S&P 500 ROE and P/E, the current S&P 500 
ROE of 24% — a record high — would point to a forward 12-month P/E of over 21x 
against today’s macro backdrop. Given current inflation and interest rates, today’s S&P 
500 multiple of 19x would be consistent with an ROE of roughly 22%, more than 200 bp 
below the current level but otherwise matching 2021 as the highest on record.

### Page 15

Earnings, rather than valuations, should continue to drive equity market upside 
from here. Our 3-, 6-, and 12-month S&P 500 return targets are +5 (8000), +9% (8300), 
and +14% (8700). These imply a P/E multiple on consensus forward EPS that remains 
close to the current level of 19x. Our economists and interest rate strategists expect 
interest rates to decline modestly in coming quarters, creating some macro upside risk to 
equity valuations. However, we expect investor uncertainty regarding the impact of AI 
on the outlook for long-term profits to persist in coming quarters, making a dramatic 
increase in valuations unlikely.

率上升至少40個基點的滾動六個月週期內，REITs的

REIT 與 10 年期公債殖利率呈負相關，主要因為受
到租賃期間長短的影響 ，其中以飯店業及倉儲設施
因租賃期間較短可當下反應租金變動 ，購物中心因
經濟表現佳 ，可支配所得增加 ，所以表現反而較佳 ；
淨租賃、醫療保健REITs表現最差，但以近期而言，

REITs受個別基本面不同，部分業者營運模式從過去
較長天期的淨租賃轉往養老住宅 ，縮短租賃期，老
年人口財富負擔能力提升，支撐老人住房市場定價

《預期地產交易價格降溫 ，4Q26交易量增長放緩 》

Green Street CPPI 數據顯示，全美商業地產價格
相較於低點已呈現微幅回升 ，8月上漲0.8%。過去
一年累計上漲5%，但美國國債收益率的上升及對借
貸成本的影響 ，恐促使購房者重新考慮支付意願 ，
預計漲勢即將降溫。依高盛統計截至8月的季度累計
地產交易量年增 18%(扣除合併 )，高於世邦魏理仕
和仲量聯行對 3Q26投資銷售額年增 13%和14%的
預期，8月份的強勁成長主要由工業地產和辦公地產
推動，而多戶住宅市場仍然落後 。工業倉儲主要受
惠供應過剩逐漸下降 ，租賃活動受到美國製造業回
流、近岸外包與第三方物流供應鏈重組支撐 ，優質
近港與門戶型物流中心空置率開始回落；辦公室物
業則呈現科技與專業服務企業偏好承租一線 A級商
辦，而不青睞 B/C級商辦 ，拆除舊型辦公室 、改建
住宅等 。多戶住宅仍在消化陽光帶 (德州、佛州等)
過剩的存量，壓抑短期租金漲幅 。受30年期固定房
貸利率在9月升至7%的高位及去年基期也高的影響 ，
預期4Q26交易量放緩。
